Appendix 0210 A – Standard Pipe Support Details

---

## 1.0 Preamble

1.1 This engineering standard is established to update without losing sight of the philosophy of the original plant design established by the Bechtel Corporation (1965) "The Basic Pipe Support Design Manual".

1.2 This standard references the existing identification system and pre-engineered components manufactured by the Grinnell Corporation. Alternate manfacturers' components may be submitted for approval by the Owner's Engineer.

## 2.0 Scope

This manual outlines the general methods used for supporting, guiding and anchoring piping systems.

## 3.0 Codes and Regulations

### 3.1 Codes

All pipe support components shall comply with the latest edition of the following codes and standards. These documents shall be deemed to form part of this specification.

- 3.1.1 Government of Alberta
  Alberta Building Code
- 3.1.2 American Society of Mechanical Engineers (ASME )
  ASME B31.1, Power Piping Code, latest edition.
  ASME B31.3, "Process Piping", latest edition.
- 3.1.3 American Society of Testing & Materials (ASTM)
  ASTM A325, High Strength Bolts for Structural Joints
- 3.1.4 Canadian Standards Association (CSA)
  CSA S16.1, Steel Structures for Buildings
  CSA G40.21, Structural Quality Steels
  CSA W47.1, Certification of Companies for Fusion Welding of Steel Structures
  CSA W48.1, Mild Steel Covered Arc-Welding Electrodes
- 3.1.5 Canadian Institute of Steel Construction (CISC)
  CISC Handbook of Steel Construction
- 3.1.6 Manufacturers Standardization Society of the Valves and Fittings Industry
  MSS SP-58, Pipe Hangers and Supports - Materials Design & Manufacture
  MSS SP-69, Pipe Hangers and Supports - Selection and Application

---

## 4.0 Definitions

### 4.1 Abnormal Conditions

Abnormal conditions, as related to requirements for piping anchor design, refer to conditions encountered in pipe lines during:

(a) A loss of cooling medium flow.
(b) Cessation of process flow while line heating facilities remain operative.
(c) Anticipatable startup and shutdown requirements, including steam out and process upsets causing temperature fluctuations.

### 4.2 Anchor Bays

Anchor bays are elevated pipe supports acting as anchors, which use cross braces to transmit thrust to the anchor bay foundations by tensile and compressive member forces.

## 5.0 Materials

### 5.1 Structural Steel

Unless otherwise specified, all standard and special pipe support brackets and their structural members shall be manufactured using the materials specified below. Alternative materials shall only be used with prior approval of the Owner's Engineer.

(a) Structural Steel CSA - G40.21
(b) Bolting ASTM A325 High Strength Bolts for Structural Joints

### 5.2 Fabrication

5.2.1 All welds to pressure containing parts shall meet the requirements of Suncor Energy Inc. STD-0903.

5.2.2 Welded pipe support attachments to coated, lined or stress relieved piping shall be affixed to the piping prior to coating, lining or stress relieving.

5.2.3 Pipe supports when specified, shall be painted in accordance with Suncor Energy Inc. STD-0214, "Painting". Shoes to be same color as pipe. Steel brackets to be same color as steelwork.

## 6.0 General Design Criteria

6.1 Pipe support systems and detail design shall comply with the appropriate piping codes.

6.2 Loading criteria for environmentally generated forces shall comply with the Alberta Building Code.

6.3 The structural design of support brackets shall comply with CSA S16.1.

6.4 Where pre-engineered components are specified, e.g., support clamps, spring hangers, etc., they shall comply with MSS SP-58 and SP-69.

6.5 Snow loads on piping shall be neglected. Thrust due to changes in line temperature shall be considered in the design of anchor bays.

6.6 Elevated pipe supports, other than anchor bays, shall not normally be designed to resist thrust due to changes in line temperature.

---

> **Table - Header — Suncor Energy STD 0210 / Pipe Supports / Rev. 8 August 2006 / Page 5 of 11** - see [source crop](tables/06-pipe-supports-02_p0004_tbl01_header-suncor-energy-std-0210-pipe-suppo.png)

6.7 Top of concrete piers for pipe supports shall be a minimum of 6 in. above finished grade or high point of paving.

6.8 Spring type supports for hangers shall be calibrated for both load and travel and shall be provided with a load indicator. Calibration plates shall be made of metal and shall be permanently fastened to the spring housing. A marker shall be provided on spring support load indicators to show both cold and hot load spring setting.

6.9 Supports shall be suitable for carrying the weight of the piping when filled with water during hydrostatic pressure testing, except where:

(a) Pneumatic testing has been approved.

(b) The specific gravity (relative density) of the process fluid exceeds 1.0.

6.10 Pipe supports shall be of structural steel or reinforced concrete.

6.11 Location of Supports

6.11.1 Support Spans

(a) The maximum sag of a pipe span shall be 1" as determined from tables or by flexibility analysis. This deflection allowance may be reduced for drainage by sloping.

(b) Large diameter thin wall lines shall be analyzed for crushing loads at support points, and reinforced as required.

6.12 Plastic pipe may be supported with standard pipe supports and hangers. There shall be no rough or sharp edges in contact with the pipe.

6.13 Coated, lined, stress relieved and alloy piping shall have clamp on type shoes and guides, unless attachment welds are made prior to such treatment.

6.14 When a carbon steel support is to be welded to an alloy pipe a spacer of compatible material shall be welded to the pipe before attaching the support.

---

## 6.15 Hanger Rods

Hanger rod loads shall be limited to those given in the following table. Where an application demands a higher stress value then specific calculations based on the CISC code shall be prepared.

> **Table - CARRYING CAPACITIES OF THREADED HOT ROLLED CARBON STEEL RODS** - see [source crop](tables/06-pipe-supports-02_p0005_tbl01_carrying-capacities-threaded-hot-rolled.png)

**CARRYING CAPACITIES OF THREADED
HOT ROLLED CARBON STEEL RODS**

| Nominal Rod Diameter Inches (") | Max. Safe Load at Temp of 650°F Pounds |
| :--- | ---: |
| 3/8 | 610 |
| ½ | 1,130 |
| 5/8 | 1,810 |
| 3/4 | 2,710 |
| 7/8 | 3,770 |
| 1 | 4,960 |
| 1¼ | 8,000 |
| 1½ | 11,630 |
| 1¾ | 15,690 |
| 2 | 20,690 |

**NOTE:**

Tabulated loads are based on an allowable tensile stress of 12,000 psi reduced by 25% using the root thread area as a base.

6.16 When a special pipe support is no more than a simple structural bracket, the design detail shall be described on the corresponding isometric or orthographic drawing. No separate detail drawing shall be made.

6.17 All spring supports shall be detailed on individual drawings. Spring data shall be shown on the drawing and is to include size, operating load, installation load and travel.

6.18 Each special and spring support shall bear reference to the orthographic or isometric drawing numbers with which it is associated.

6.19 Piping to equipment requiring removal for maintenance, including control valve assemblies, shall be supported so that temporary supports are not required during removal.

6.20 Support of Uninsulated Piping. Uninsulated lines shall rest directly on the support except shoes shall be provided on lines subject to excessive or continuous movement. Half pipe cradle supports or sleeve anchors fitting concentrically around the pipe, shall be employed where required.

6.21 Hot service insulated lines shall be supported with steel support shoes welded, except as specified in 5.2 and 6.14, to the pipe as shown in the pipe support details.

6.22 Shoes and/or guides shall be provided on lines subject to excessive or continuous movement.

---

6.23 Piping anchors shall be designed for the forces developed under normal or abnormal conditions, whichever governs. The design of structures anchoring more than one line shall accommodate the more stringent combination of the forces due to abnormal conditions on the following basis:

> **Table - Total number of lines at an anchor structure — Percent of lines abnormal conditions to be used in design** - see [XLSX](tables/06-pipe-supports-02_p0006_tbl01_anchor-structure-lines-abnormal-percent.xlsx) and [source crop](tables/06-pipe-supports-02_p0006_tbl01_anchor-structure-lines-abnormal-percent.png)

| Total number of lines at an anchor structure that could have abnormal conditions: | Percent of lines abnormal conditions to be used in design: |
| :--- | ---: |
| 1 | 100% |
| 2 to 4 | 50% |
| over 4 | 25% |

**NOTE:**

(1) Use next larger whole number of lines indicated. Generally select largest lines, providing thrust loads are in the same direction.

6.24 The following coefficients of static friction shall be used to determine forces at sliding surfaces:

> **Table - Coefficients of static friction for sliding surfaces** - see [XLSX](tables/06-pipe-supports-02_p0006_tbl02_static-friction-coefficients-sliding-sur.xlsx) and [source crop](tables/06-pipe-supports-02_p0006_tbl02_static-friction-coefficients-sliding-sur.png)

| :--- | ---: |
| PTFE on PTFE | 0.10 |
| PTFE on Stainless Steel | 0.10 |
| Steel on Steel | 0.40 |
| Steel on Concrete | 0.45 |

6.25 All spring hangers and supports shall be adjusted in the field, after unit start-up, in accordance with the manufacturer's instructions. Manufacturer's name plate shall indicate hot and cold set points.

6.26 Variable spring hangers and supports shall be provided with a scale showing the range of working load and a means of adjustment. Constant support spring hangers shall be provided with the calibration load clearly indicated, a scale showing the range of movement and an accurate means of adjustment. Calibration plates shall be made of metal and shall be permanently attached to the spring housing.

6.27 Where Teflon bearing pads are used at pipe supports, the top pad shall overlap the bottom pad by a minimum of ½" in both cold and hot positions. Alternatively, a cover shall be provided to prevent dirt from getting on the pads.

6.28 Where Graphite slide plates are used at pipe supports, the proposed method of bonding the graphite to the pipe shoe and/or pipe support shall be approved by the Owner's Engineer. Approval to be based on proof of satisfactory performance of the proposed bonding method in similar applications.

6.29 The designs of pipe supports, anchors, and restraints employing PTFE or other low friction materials or bearing surfaces shall be reviewed by the Owner's Engineer.

6.30 Dead-ended extensions of piping shall not be used in lieu of dummy pipe supports, or equivalent structural members, to span the distance from piping runs to the nearest support structure. Dummy pipe supports, where used,

---

shall be provided with a low point drain hole. For horizontal installations, the drain hole shall be located near the point of attachment to the piping component. Where stagnant or confined areas of piping are unavoidable due to piping layout requirements, the location of all such piping components shall be documented to the Owner's Engineer for corrosion monitoring.

6.31 Flexibility analysis of piping shall be per ASME B31.1 or ASME B31.3 as appropriate. For detailed Suncor Energy Inc. requirements, see STD-0201, Attachment A. The metal temperature for each condition shall be the temperature of the contained fluid. Metal temperature for lines without external insulation may be reduced by 5%. Temperature differentials for each condition shall be determined as follows:

> **Table - Metal Temperature vs Temperature Differential** - see [XLSX](tables/06-pipe-supports-02_p0007_tbl01_metal-temperature-vs-temperature-differe.xlsx) and [source crop](tables/06-pipe-supports-02_p0007_tbl01_metal-temperature-vs-temperature-differe.png)

| Metal Temperature | Temperature Differential (2) |
| :--- | :--- |
| Above 120°F (1) | Metal temperature minus average minimum winter dry bulb temperature |
| Below 120°F (1), but above average minimum winter dry bulb temperature | 120°F (1) minus average minimum winter dry bulb temperature |
| Below average minimum winter dry bulb temperature | 120°F (1) minus metal bulb temperature |

**NOTES:**

(1) The 120°F temperature is considered the maximum metal temperature resulting from solar radiation with no flow in the line.

(2) Under certain conditions of dual service, the fluid temperature range may be both above 120°F and below average minimum winter dry bulb temperature.

6.32 Lateral stability for lines outside of process unit limits. Axial thrust loads in long, horizontal runs of pipe due to static friction resistance to thermal expansion at the support points shall not exceed the critical buckling load. If necessary, lateral guides shall be used to give stability to the line.

6.33 Friction Effects. No credit shall be taken for reduced thermal expansion movements or reduced flexibility stresses due to the elastic axial compression resulting from support point friction forces.

6.34 Lap joint flanges. The combined longitudinal stress in the pipe where attached to the lap-joint stub-end resulting from pressure, weight, and thermal expansion, shall not exceed 0.8 times the ASME B31.3/B31.1 basic allowable stress of the lap or pipe material at the pipe design temperature.

6.35 Venturi gate valves. Flexibility stresses in the valve body shall be determined based on the minimum section modulus of the valve body (excluding ribs and bosses). The stress range calculated in this manner shall not exceed the allowable stress range for the valve material.

6.36 Expansion bends and offsets shall be used. Expansion joints or flexible metal hose within all process unit areas, or designated "potential" fire areas outside process unit areas, may be used with owner's approval.

## 7.0 References

7.1 See the drawings listed in Appendix 0210A for the various supports referred to herein.

## 8.0 Vertical Lines Supported From Towers

8.1 Lines shall be supported as high as possible to minimize differential expansion due to temperature.

8.2 Lines shall be guided at sufficient intervals to prevent vibration or sway due to wind. Excessive loads on guides and supports shall result in more guides or supports being used and/or reinforcement added to vessel.

## 9.0 Pressure Vessel Pipe Supports

9.1 Welding to pressure vessels is not permitted. The vessel piping shall be supported from clips furnished by vessel fabricator, platforms or surrounding structure.

## 10.0 Control Valve Manifolds

10.1 Check for guide requirements of manifolds supported from above.

## 11.0 Rotating Machinery

11.1 The use of cold spring for piping systems which connect to rotating equipment is prohibited.

11.2 The use of couplings or expansion joints in the suction or discharge lines of pumps in cooling water service is acceptable. They shall be located on the pump side of the pump suction or discharge block valve, as appropriate. All pressure thrust loads shall be absorbed by anchors or tie rods, and not by the coupling or expansion joint. The type of coupling and piping systems design shall be reviewed with the Owner's Engineer.

## 12.0 Piping at Pumps

12.1 Supports for pump piping shall be designed to take the valve and pipe loads when pumps are removed for repairs and to prevent bending moments being transmitted to the pump nozzles due to eccentric loading.

12.2 Where frost heave or grade settlement may be encountered, supports from grade must be mounted on the pump foundation.

## 13.0 Steam Turbine Piping

13.1 Steam turbines used for pump drivers are manufactured in two types: Casing-mounted and centre-mounted. The casing-mounted type has support lugs located near the casing bottom. The nozzles and shaft expand up from the lugs at exhaust steam temperatures.

---

13.2 The centre-mounted type has a pedestal support under the shaft bearing. The pedestal temperature varies from half that of the exhaust steam temperature at the bearing to ambient at its base. The shaft expands up slightly from the heat in the pedestal. Since the nozzles are usually located below the shaft, their expansion is down from the shaft at exhaust steam temperatures. Their net thermal movement is down.

13.3 When expansion joints are used to eliminate grade settlement or frost heave hazards, the risers must be anchored laterally.

## 14.0 Reciprocating Compressor Piping

14.1 Piping and surge bottles for reciprocating compressors often present vibration problems. Bottles mounted unsymmetrically or to one side should be firmly supported from the compressor frame or grade to restrain the vibrating loads. To be effective, these restraints should limit the motion to an over-all amplitude of not over .001" or .002".

14.2 Provisions for thermal movement of the bottles and connected piping must be made. Vibrating piping systems can be restrained with anchors or lateral guides when passing over stanchion beams or other rigid supports or by means of vibration dampeners. Lines subject to vibration should not be supported by hangers.

14.3 Piping systems containing cantilevered 90 degree bends are very troublesome and must be avoided. Piping must be supported at each change in direction.

## 15.0 Heater Piping

15.1 Heater piping and supports vary with the type of heater. Heaters can be roughly classified as cylindrical and rectangular.

15.2 Cylindrical heaters have vertical tubes located concentrically inside a thin steel shell with nozzles at the top. Piping from the nozzles drops down the outside, usually in a flat bank. Due to large heater diameter and thin shell, all supports from heater require shell reinforcing.

15.3 Rectangular heaters usually have horizontal tubes inside a thin steel enclosure and a structural steel frame. Their nozzles are located at about midway levels and can take only small (500# average) forces from connecting piping. Special anchors are usually required to protect these nozzles and internal piping.

## 16.0 Piping In or About Structures

16.1 Horizontal pipe runs are supported from beams with trapeze hangers Types L9 and L10 when multiple; by Type H-1, when single or from structure columns by Types B-1 thru B-3 brackets.

16.2 Vertical runs outside the structure are supported and guided from support beams similar to those shown on Standard drawings B11 thru B13.

---

## 17.0 Relief Valve Stacks

17.1 Relief valve stacks venting to atmosphere may be categorized into short (under 6' - 0") and long (up to 20' - 0"). Short stacks do not need special supporting consideration unless relieving high pressures

17.2 Long stacks require supports to prevent pipe load and relief forces for overstressing the nozzle. Guides must allow vertical thermal movement but prevent rotation due to vertical loads and forces and thus take the moment off the nozzle. These are classed as special supports.

## 18.0 Pipeway Piping

18.1 Pipeway piping may be divided into short and long pipe runs. The short runs are generally process lines between pieces of equipment. Long pipe runs, while not limited to any one area, are characteristic of off plot piping.

18.2 All long pipe runs should be anchored to prevent creeping. See anchor details Types DA1 thru DA11. Hot lines expand away from their anchors and cold lines contract towards them.

18.3 The locations of pipe anchors, critical guides and expansion loops are designated by the stress analysis group.

## 19.0 Pipe Support Shoes

19.1 Insulated lines are provided with shoes to allow movement over the supports.

19.2 The shoes must be sized and located properly to permit movement without running off the supports. Should the shoes run off the supports, followed by a line temperature reversal, the supports could be broken or the line ruptured.

19.3 All hot or cold insulated lines, irrespective of size, shall be provided with shoes at all supports.

19.4 Shoe lengths are 12", 18", 24", 30", 36" and 40", as per Standard Detail Drawings.

NOTES

1. ALL STRUCTURAL MEMBERS ARE METRIC. DIMENSIONS ARE IN INCHES.
2. USE THIS SUPPORT WHERE WELDING TO THE PIPE IS NOT FEASIBLE.
3. GAP TOLERANCE IS +1/8", -0.
4. MAXIMUM PIPE TEMPERATURE NOT TO EXCEED 200°F.
5. TORQUE TOLERANCE +0 FT-lb, -0.3 FT-lb.

**NOTES:**

1. ALL STRUCTURAL MEMBERS AND METAL HARDWARE ARE IN GENERAL MILD STEEL. FOR NPS 1 CLAMP PART OF THE PIPE CLAMP, USE MALLEABLE IRON; FOR NPS 1-1/2 AND ABOVE, USE CARBON STEEL.

2. STAINLESS STEEL PIPES AND FITTINGS. SUPPORT MAY BE USED ON CARBON STEEL, STAINLESS STEEL, OR ALLOY PIPING.

3. THE SUPPORT SHALL RESIST PIPE AXIAL THRUST.

4. WELD TO PIPE SURFACE THE SUPPORT LEG AS REQUIRED.

5. WELD THICKNESS SHALL NOT EXCEED THE PIPE WALL THICKNESS.

6. WELD SIZES ARE REQUIRED; THEY SHALL BE EQUAL WELD ALL AROUND UNLESS NOTED.

7. PIPING AND SUPPORTS ARE MEASURED FROM CENTRELINE (CL) OF PIPE.

8. PACKING SETS ARE NOT INCLUDED SHOWN IN THIS DRAWING; IF REQUIRED, TO REDUCE LOCAL STRESSES IN THE PIPE WALL.

THE DUMMY SUPPORT IS WELDED TO THE TRIMMED NECK THICKNESS OF THE PIPE USING 1/4" OR SMALLER FILLET WELD.

**NOTES:**

1. ALL STRUCTURAL MEMBERS ARE ASTM A36/A36M AND ARE WELDED, AS REQUIRED, TO PIPES AND EACH OTHER. ALL WELDS SHALL BE PROPER FIT AND OF THE PROPER QUALITY FOR THE WORK BEING PERFORMED.
2. PIPE SUPPORTS SHALL NOT BE ATTACHED TO INSTRUMENT PIPING. TRUNNIONS NEED NOT BE WELDED TO PIPE.
3. TRUNNIONS SHALL BE MINIMUM SCHEDULE TO MATCH PIPE. PROPER FITTING QUALITY IS REQUIRED.
4. PIPE WELD DONE USING D1.1 OR EQUIVALENT WELDING PROCEDURE QUALIFIED TO APPLICABLE DESIGN CODE. WELDER TO BE QUALIFIED TO D1.1 TO PERFORM THE WELD.
5. THE LOAD LOAD IS THE FULL LOAD INCLUDING SEISMIC LOADING USING THE STATIC LOAD APPROACH.
6. THE SUPPORT LOAD IS THE FULL LOAD INCLUDING SEISMIC LOAD WHICH THE SUPPORT IS DESIGNED.

## ELEVATION DETAIL "A"

Two nuts on bottom, P1 DIA, supporting pipe, L76X76X7.9, nut each side, P2 DIA, loose fit U-bolt, shown by field, 2 nuts (TYP.), STD loose fit (TYP.)

## ELEVATION DETAIL "B"

Loose fit U-bolt, nut each side, P2 DIA, shown by field, L76X76X7.9, 2 nuts, supporting pipe, P1 DIA, 2 (TYP.)

## ELEVATION DETAIL "C"

Supporting pipes, P1 DIA, P3 DIA, loose fit (TYP.), P2 DIA, STD (TYP.), L102x102x9.5, 2¼" MAX

## ELEVATION DETAIL "D"

Loose fit U-bolt, P1 DIA, P2 DIA, supporting pipe, L76X76X7.9, 24" MAX

## ELEVATION DETAIL "E"

P1 DIA, P2 DIA, 1¼×1×3/8 shear lug (same mat'l as pipe) (TYP.), STD tight fit (TYP.), tight fit U-bolt (TYP.), as pipe (TYP.), supporting pipe, 24" MAX, C200X17.0

## ELEVATION DETAIL "F"

P3 DIA, 1¼×1×3/8 shear lug (same mat'l as pipe) (TYP.), STD tight fit (TYP.), tight fit U-bolt, P1 DIA, P2 DIA, supporting pipe, C200X17.0

## NOTES

1. ALL DIMENSIONS ARE IN INCHES, ALL STRUCTURAL MEMBERS ARE METRIC.
2. ALL STRUCTURAL MEMBERS MATERIAL TO BE CSA-G40.21-300W.
3. U-BOLT HOLES TO BE LOCATED ON CAGE.
4. U-BOLT HOLES TO BE 1/8" LARGER THAN U-BOLT DIAMETER.
5. U-BOLTS TO BE ASTM F1137 GALVANIZED OR EQUAL WITH 2/W NUTS AND LOCKNUTS.
6. U-BOLTS FOR PIPE SIZES OF 6" PIPES OR LARGER AND HAVE A DESIGN TEMPERATURE OF 212°F OR LESS, THE RELATIVE MOVEMENTS BETWEEN SUPPORTING PIPE AND SUPPORTED PIPE SHALL BE REVIEWED BY THE STRESS ANALYST PRIOR TO USING THIS STANDARD.

**NOTES:**

1. THIS CONNECTION IS SUITABLE FOR WELDING TO EXISTING OR WELDED FABRICATED PIPE SUPPORTS AND STRUCTURAL STEEL.
2. ALL WELDING TO CONFORM TO AWS SPECIFICATIONS UNLESS OTHERWISE SPECIFIED.

6. T 1/4 FROM 4-1/2 TO 1/2 INSIDE OF PIPE DIMENSIONS OF STEMS SHALL COMPLY TO THE APPLICABLE CODE. DOUBLE CONNECTION TYPE IS AVAILABLE ON SPECIAL ORDER.
7. ALL STRUCTURAL MEMBERS ARE METAL. THICKNESS AND MATERIAL ARE PROVIDED.
8. WELD SIZE 3/8 TO 3/4 WHEN USING BOTTOM CONNECTION 5.

SLIDING SURFACE (NOM. 3)

NOTES:

1. FOR STRUCTURAL MEMBERS AND FITTINGS - DIMENSIONS AND MATERIALS SPECIFIED PER VESSEL FABRICATION. ALL SUPPORTS ARE DESIGNED TO SUPPORT FULL WEIGHT OF PIPE, FITTINGS, FLUID AND INSULATION.
2. SUPPORT FABRICATION TO CONFORM TO ATTACHMENT BOLTS DETAILS ON VESSEL.
3. TEFLON OR EQUAL SLIDING SURFACE TO BE PROVIDED AT PIPE SHOE LOCATION.
4. 1" = 1'-0" MINIMUM VERTICAL LOAD FOR ALL SUPPORTS SHALL BE EQUAL TO WEIGHT OF PIPE + INSULATION + FLUID + FITTINGS TIMES A SAFETY FACTOR OF 2.
5. A MINIMUM OF TWO (2) SUPPORT ANCHOR BOLTS TO BE PROVIDED.
6. WHERE APPLICABLE, SLIDING SURFACE SHALL BE THE FULL WIDTH OF THE SUPPORT, INCLUDING FRICTION FACTOR CONSIDERATIONS FOR HOT LINES.

## NOTES:

1. ALL DIMENSIONS ARE IN INCHES.
2. ALL STRUCTURAL MEMBERS ARE METRIC.
3. WELD THICKNESS SHALL NOT EXCEED THE PARENT PIPE WALL THICKNESS.
